```
69	pub fn make_parser_for(lang: Lang) -> tree_sitter::Parser {
70	    let mut parser = tree_sitter::Parser::new();
71	    let language: tree_sitter::Language = match lang {
72	        Lang::Rust => tree_sitter_rust::LANGUAGE.into(),
73	        Lang::JavaScript => tree_sitter_javascript::LANGUAGE.into(),
74	        Lang::TypeScript => tree_sitter_typescript::LANGUAGE_TYPESCRIPT.into(),
75	    };
76	    parser
77	        .set_language(&language)
78	        .expect("failed to load grammar");
79	    parser
80	}
81	
82	pub fn make_parser() -> tree_sitter::Parser {
83	    make_parser_for(Lang::Rust)
84	}
85	
86	pub fn lang_config(lang: Lang) -> &'static LangConfig {
87	    match lang {
88	        Lang::Rust => &RUST_CONFIG,
89	        Lang::JavaScript => &JS_CONFIG,
90	        Lang::TypeScript => &TS_CONFIG,
91	    }
92	}
93	
94	pub fn parse_source_tree_for(source: &str, lang: Lang) -> Result<tree_sitter::Tree, &'static str> {
95	    let tree = make_parser_for(lang)
96	        .parse(source, None)
97	        .ok_or("parse returned None")?;
98	    if tree.root_node().has_error() {
99	        return Err("parse error");
100	    }
101	    Ok(tree)
102	}
103	
104	pub fn parse_source_tree(source: &str) -> Result<tree_sitter::Tree, &'static str> {
105	    parse_source_tree_for(source, Lang::Rust)
106	}

108	pub fn children_of(node: tree_sitter::Node) -> Vec<tree_sitter::Node> {
109	    let mut cursor = node.walk();
110	    node.children(&mut cursor).collect()
111	}

113	pub fn extract_functions(
114	    node: tree_sitter::Node,
115	    source: &str,
116	    file: &str,
117	    functions: &mut Vec<FunctionInfo>,
118	) {
119	    extract_functions_with_config(node, source, file, &RUST_CONFIG, functions);
120	}
121	
122	pub fn extract_functions_with_config(
123	    node: tree_sitter::Node,
124	    source: &str,
125	    file: &str,
126	    config: &LangConfig,
127	    functions: &mut Vec<FunctionInfo>,
128	) {
129	    match config.lang {
130	        Lang::Rust => extract_rust_functions(node, source, file, functions),
131	        Lang::JavaScript | Lang::TypeScript => {
132	            extract_jsts_functions(node, source, file, config, functions)
133	        }
134	    }
135	}
136	
137	fn extract_rust_functions(
138	    node: tree_sitter::Node,
139	    source: &str,
140	    file: &str,
141	    functions: &mut Vec<FunctionInfo>,
142	) {
143	    match node.kind() {
144	        "function_item" => {
145	            functions.push(make_function_info(node, source, file, &RUST_CONFIG));
146	        }
147	        "let_declaration" => {
148	            extract_rust_let_declaration(node, source, file, functions);
149	        }
150	        _ => {
151	            for child in children_of(node) {
152	                extract_rust_functions(child, source, file, functions);
153	            }
154	        }
155	    }
156	}
157	
158	pub fn extract_let_declaration(
159	    node: tree_sitter::Node,
160	    source: &str,
161	    file: &str,
162	    functions: &mut Vec<FunctionInfo>,
163	) {
164	    extract_rust_let_declaration(node, source, file, functions);
165	}
166	
167	fn extract_rust_let_declaration(
168	    node: tree_sitter::Node,
169	    source: &str,
170	    file: &str,
171	    functions: &mut Vec<FunctionInfo>,
172	) {
173	    if let Some(val) = find_child_by_field(node, "value")
174	        && val.kind() == "closure_expression"
175	    {
176	        functions.push(make_function_info(node, source, file, &RUST_CONFIG));
177	    }
178	    for child in children_of(node) {
179	        if child.kind() != "closure_expression" {
180	            extract_rust_functions(child, source, file, functions);
181	        }
182	    }
183	}
184	
185	fn extract_jsts_export_statement(
186	    node: tree_sitter::Node,
187	    source: &str,
188	    file: &str,
189	    config: &LangConfig,
190	    functions: &mut Vec<FunctionInfo>,
191	) {
192	    let fn_decls: Vec<_> = children_of(node)
193	        .into_iter()
194	        .filter(|c| c.kind() == "function_declaration")
195	        .collect();
196	    if fn_decls.is_empty() {
197	        for child in children_of(node) {
198	            extract_jsts_functions(child, source, file, config, functions);
199	        }
200	    } else {
201	        for child in fn_decls {
202	            functions.push(make_function_info(child, source, file, config));
203	        }
204	    }
205	}
206	
207	fn extract_jsts_lexical_declaration(
208	    node: tree_sitter::Node,
209	    source: &str,
210	    file: &str,
211	    config: &LangConfig,
212	    functions: &mut Vec<FunctionInfo>,
213	) {
214	    for child in children_of(node) {
215	        if child.kind() == "variable_declarator" {
216	            extract_jsts_variable_declarator(child, source, file, config, functions);
217	        }
218	    }
219	}
220	
221	fn extract_jsts_class_body(
222	    node: tree_sitter::Node,
223	    source: &str,
224	    file: &str,
225	    config: &LangConfig,
226	    functions: &mut Vec<FunctionInfo>,
227	) {
228	    for child in children_of(node) {
229	        if child.kind() == "method_definition" {
230	            functions.push(make_function_info(child, source, file, config));
231	        }
232	    }
233	}
234	
235	fn try_extract_jsts_node(
236	    node: tree_sitter::Node,
237	    source: &str,
238	    file: &str,
239	    config: &LangConfig,
240	    functions: &mut Vec<FunctionInfo>,
241	) -> bool {
242	    match node.kind() {
243	        "function_declaration" => functions.push(make_function_info(node, source, file, config)),
244	        "export_statement" => extract_jsts_export_statement(node, source, file, config, functions),
245	        "lexical_declaration" => {
246	            extract_jsts_lexical_declaration(node, source, file, config, functions)
247	        }
248	        "class_body" => extract_jsts_class_body(node, source, file, config, functions),
249	        _ => return false,
250	    }
251	    true
252	}
253	
254	fn extract_jsts_functions(
255	    node: tree_sitter::Node,
256	    source: &str,
257	    file: &str,
258	    config: &LangConfig,
259	    functions: &mut Vec<FunctionInfo>,
260	) {
261	    if !try_extract_jsts_node(node, source, file, config, functions) {
262	        for child in children_of(node) {
263	            extract_jsts_functions(child, source, file, config, functions);
264	        }
265	    }
266	}
267	
268	fn extract_jsts_variable_declarator(
269	    node: tree_sitter::Node,
270	    source: &str,
271	    file: &str,
272	    config: &LangConfig,
273	    functions: &mut Vec<FunctionInfo>,
274	) {
275	    if let Some(val) = find_child_by_field(node, "value")
276	        && val.kind() == "arrow_function"
277	    {
278	        functions.push(make_function_info(node, source, file, config));
279	    }
280	}

282	pub fn find_child_by_field<'a>(
283	    node: tree_sitter::Node<'a>,
284	    field: &str,
285	) -> Option<tree_sitter::Node<'a>> {
286	    let mut cursor = node.walk();
287	    for child in node.children(&mut cursor) {
288	        if child.kind() == field {
289	            return Some(child);
290	        }
291	    }
292	    node.child_by_field_name(field)
293	}
294	
295	pub fn make_function_info(
296	    node: tree_sitter::Node,
297	    source: &str,
298	    file: &str,
299	    config: &LangConfig,
300	) -> FunctionInfo {
301	    let start_line = node.start_position().row + 1;
302	    let end_line = node.end_position().row + 1;
303	    let hashes = collect_subtree_hashes(node, source, config);
304	    FunctionInfo {
305	        file: file.to_string(),
306	        start_line,
307	        end_line,
308	        node_hashes: hashes,
309	    }
310	}
311	
312	fn normalize_node_text(node: tree_sitter::Node, source: &str, config: &LangConfig) -> String {
313	    let kind = node.kind();
314	    if config.identifier_kinds.contains(&kind) {
315	        return "_ID".to_string();
316	    }
317	    if config.literal_kinds.contains(&kind) {
318	        return "_LIT".to_string();
319	    }
320	    let start = node.start_byte();
321	    let end = node.end_byte();
322	    source[start..end].to_string()
323	}
324	
325	pub fn build_normalized_subtree(
326	    node: tree_sitter::Node,
327	    source: &str,
328	    config: &LangConfig,
329	) -> String {
330	    if node.child_count() == 0 {
331	        return normalize_node_text(node, source, config);
332	    }
333	    let mut parts = vec![format!("({}:", node.kind())];
334	    let mut cursor = node.walk();
335	    for child in node.children(&mut cursor) {
336	        parts.push(build_normalized_subtree(child, source, config));
337	    }
338	    parts.push(")".to_string());
339	    parts.join("")
340	}
341	
342	fn hash_str(s: &str) -> u64 {
343	    use std::hash::{Hash, Hasher};
344	    let mut h = std::collections::hash_map::DefaultHasher::new();
345	    s.hash(&mut h);
346	    h.finish()
347	}
348	
349	fn collect_subtree_hashes(node: tree_sitter::Node, source: &str, config: &LangConfig) -> Vec<u64> {
350	    let mut hashes = Vec::new();
351	    collect_hashes_recursive(node, source, config, &mut hashes);
352	    hashes.sort_unstable();
353	    hashes
354	}
355	
356	fn collect_hashes_recursive(
357	    node: tree_sitter::Node,
358	    source: &str,
359	    config: &LangConfig,
360	    hashes: &mut Vec<u64>,
361	) {
362	    let subtree = build_normalized_subtree(node, source, config);
363	    hashes.push(hash_str(&subtree));
364	    let mut cursor = node.walk();
365	    for child in node.children(&mut cursor) {
366	        collect_hashes_recursive(child, source, config, hashes);
367	    }
368	}

455	    #[test]
456	    fn js_twin_functions_same_structure_different_ids_score_high() {
457	        let src_a = "function accumulate_sum(a, b) {\n  let sum = a + b;\n  let extra = sum * 2;\n  let more = extra + a;\n  let result = more + b;\n  return result;\n}\n";
458	        let src_b = "function accumulate_sum(x, y) {\n  let total = x + y;\n  let extra = total * 2;\n  let more = extra + x;\n  let result = more + y;\n  return result;\n}\n";
459	        let mut parser = make_js_parser();
460	        let tree_a = parser.parse(src_a, None).unwrap();
461	        let tree_b = parser.parse(src_b, None).unwrap();
462	        let mut funcs_a = Vec::new();
463	        let mut funcs_b = Vec::new();
464	        extract_functions_with_config(tree_a.root_node(), src_a, "a.js", &JS_CONFIG, &mut funcs_a);
465	        extract_functions_with_config(tree_b.root_node(), src_b, "b.js", &JS_CONFIG, &mut funcs_b);
466	        assert_eq!(funcs_a.len(), 1, "expected 1 function in a.js");
467	        assert_eq!(funcs_b.len(), 1, "expected 1 function in b.js");
468	        let score = crate::jaccard(&funcs_a[0].node_hashes, &funcs_b[0].node_hashes);
469	        assert!(score >= 0.82, "expected score >= 0.82, got {}", score);
470	    }

533	    #[test]
534	    fn rust_and_js_functions_with_same_structure_do_not_pair() {
535	        // Rust and JS use different grammar node kinds, so hashes differ.
536	        // The accumulate_sum pattern should not score >= threshold between grammars.
537	        let rust_src = "pub fn accumulate_sum(a: i32, b: i32) -> i32 {\n    let sum = a + b;\n    let extra = sum * 2;\n    let more = extra + a;\n    let result = more + b;\n    result\n}\n";
538	        let js_src = "function accumulate_sum(a, b) {\n  let sum = a + b;\n  let extra = sum * 2;\n  let more = extra + a;\n  let result = more + b;\n  return result;\n}\n";
539	        let mut rust_parser = make_parser();
540	        let mut js_parser = make_js_parser();
541	        let rust_tree = rust_parser.parse(rust_src, None).unwrap();
542	        let js_tree = js_parser.parse(js_src, None).unwrap();
543	        let mut rust_funcs = Vec::new();
544	        let mut js_funcs = Vec::new();
545	        extract_functions(rust_tree.root_node(), rust_src, "a.rs", &mut rust_funcs);
546	        extract_functions_with_config(
547	            js_tree.root_node(),
548	            js_src,
549	            "b.js",
550	            &JS_CONFIG,
551	            &mut js_funcs,
552	        );
553	        assert_eq!(rust_funcs.len(), 1);
554	        assert_eq!(js_funcs.len(), 1);
555	        let score = crate::jaccard(&rust_funcs[0].node_hashes, &js_funcs[0].node_hashes);
556	        // Cross-language pairs should not score >= 0.82
557	        assert!(
558	            score < 0.82,
559	            "cross-language pair should score < 0.82, got {}",
560	            score
561	        );
562	    }
```

```
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Gherkin mutation runner adapter for drywall.
# Persistent worker: reads JSON job requests from stdin, writes JSON responses to stdout.
# Diagnostics go to stderr.

set -euo pipefail

SCRIPT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
PROJECT_ROOT="$(cd "$SCRIPT_DIR/../.." && pwd)"
ORIG_TEST="$PROJECT_ROOT/tests/scaffold_cli_invocation_acceptance_test.rs"
MUTATION_TEST="$PROJECT_ROOT/tests/mutation_acceptance_test.rs"

echo "runner-adapter started, project_root=$PROJECT_ROOT" >&2

cleanup() {
    rm -f "$MUTATION_TEST"
}
trap cleanup EXIT

# Generate into a temp dir then copy to tests/ with the mutation-specific name
TMPDIR_BASE="$PROJECT_ROOT/tmp/runner-adapter-gen"
mkdir -p "$TMPDIR_BASE"

while IFS= read -r line; do
    id=$(python3 -c "import sys,json; d=json.loads(sys.argv[1]); print(d['id'])" "$line")
    feature_json=$(python3 -c "import sys,json; d=json.loads(sys.argv[1]); print(d['feature_json'])" "$line")

    echo "runner-adapter: job id=$id" >&2

    start_ns=$(python3 -c "import time; print(int(time.monotonic_ns()))")
```

```
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Gherkin mutation runner adapter for drywall.
# Persistent worker: reads JSON job requests from stdin, writes JSON responses to stdout.
# Diagnostics go to stderr.

set -euo pipefail

SCRIPT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
PROJECT_ROOT="$(cd "$SCRIPT_DIR/../.." && pwd)"
ORIG_TEST="$PROJECT_ROOT/tests/scaffold_cli_invocation_acceptance_test.rs"
MUTATION_TEST="$PROJECT_ROOT/tests/mutation_acceptance_test.rs"

echo "runner-adapter started, project_root=$PROJECT_ROOT" >&2

cleanup() {
    rm -f "$MUTATION_TEST"
}
trap cleanup EXIT

# Generate into a temp dir then copy to tests/ with the mutation-specific name
TMPDIR_BASE="$PROJECT_ROOT/tmp/runner-adapter-gen"
mkdir -p "$TMPDIR_BASE"

while IFS= read -r line; do
    id=$(python3 -c "import sys,json; d=json.loads(sys.argv[1]); print(d['id'])" "$line")
    feature_json=$(python3 -c "import sys,json; d=json.loads(sys.argv[1]); print(d['feature_json'])" "$line")

    echo "runner-adapter: job id=$id" >&2

    start_ns=$(python3 -c "import time; print(int(time.monotonic_ns()))")

    outcome="test_success"
    output=""
    err_text=""

    GEN_DIR="$TMPDIR_BASE/$id"
    mkdir -p "$GEN_DIR/metadata"

    # Generate into GEN_DIR first to see the output filename
    if ! regen_out=$(python3 "$SCRIPT_DIR/generate_entrypoint.py" "$feature_json" "$GEN_DIR" 2>&1); then
        outcome="infrastructure_error"
        err_text="generate_entrypoint failed: $regen_out"
    else
        gen_file=$(find "$GEN_DIR" -name "*_acceptance_test.rs" | head -1)
        if [ -z "$gen_file" ]; then
            outcome="infrastructure_error"
            err_text="no generated acceptance test found in $GEN_DIR"
        else
            # Rewrite include paths for tests/ location
            # Original paths in generated file are relative to GEN_DIR;
            # recompute relative paths for MUTATION_TEST in tests/
            tests_dir="$PROJECT_ROOT/tests"
            acceptance_dir="$PROJECT_ROOT/acceptance"
            runtime_rel=$(python3 -c "import os; print(os.path.relpath('$acceptance_dir/runtime/mod.rs', '$tests_dir'))")
            steps_rel=$(python3 -c "import os; print(os.path.relpath('$acceptance_dir/steps/scaffold_cli_steps.rs', '$tests_dir'))")

            # Replace the include paths in the generated file
            sed -e "s|include!(\"[^\"]*runtime/mod.rs\")|include!(\"$runtime_rel\")|g" \
                -e "s|include!(\"[^\"]*scaffold_cli_steps.rs\")|include!(\"$steps_rel\")|g" \
                "$gen_file" > "$MUTATION_TEST"

            if run_out=$(cd "$PROJECT_ROOT" && cargo test --test mutation_acceptance_test 2>&1); then
                outcome="test_success"
                output="$run_out"
            else
                outcome="test_failure"
                output="$run_out"
            fi
        fi
    fi

    rm -rf "$GEN_DIR"

    end_ns=$(python3 -c "import time; print(int(time.monotonic_ns()))")
    duration=$(( end_ns - start_ns ))

    python3 -c "
import json, sys
print(json.dumps({
    'id': sys.argv[1],
    'outcome': sys.argv[2],
    'output': sys.argv[3],
    'error': sys.argv[4],
    'duration': int(sys.argv[5]),
}))" "$id" "$outcome" "$output" "$err_text" "$duration"

done
```

```
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Gherkin mutation runner adapter for drywall cli_surface feature.
# Persistent worker: reads JSON job requests from stdin, writes JSON responses to stdout.
# Diagnostics go to stderr.

set -euo pipefail

SCRIPT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
PROJECT_ROOT="$(cd "$SCRIPT_DIR/../.." && pwd)"
MUTATION_TEST="$PROJECT_ROOT/tests/mutation_acceptance_test.rs"

echo "runner-adapter-cli-surface started, project_root=$PROJECT_ROOT" >&2

cleanup() {
    rm -f "$MUTATION_TEST"
}
trap cleanup EXIT

# Generate into a temp dir then copy to tests/ with the mutation-specific name
TMPDIR_BASE="$PROJECT_ROOT/tmp/runner-adapter-cli-surface-gen"
mkdir -p "$TMPDIR_BASE"

while IFS= read -r line; do
    id=$(python3 -c "import sys,json; d=json.loads(sys.argv[1]); print(d['id'])" "$line")
    feature_json=$(python3 -c "import sys,json; d=json.loads(sys.argv[1]); print(d['feature_json'])" "$line")

    echo "runner-adapter-cli-surface: job id=$id" >&2

    start_ns=$(python3 -c "import time; print(int(time.monotonic_ns()))")

    outcome="test_success"
    output=""
    err_text=""

    GEN_DIR="$TMPDIR_BASE/$id"
    mkdir -p "$GEN_DIR/metadata"

    # Generate into GEN_DIR first to see the output filename
    if ! regen_out=$(python3 "$SCRIPT_DIR/generate_entrypoint.py" "$feature_json" "$GEN_DIR" 2>&1); then
        outcome="infrastructure_error"
        err_text="generate_entrypoint failed: $regen_out"
    else
        gen_file=$(find "$GEN_DIR" -name "*_acceptance_test.rs" | head -1)
        if [ -z "$gen_file" ]; then
            outcome="infrastructure_error"
            err_text="no generated acceptance test found in $GEN_DIR"
        else
            tests_dir="$PROJECT_ROOT/tests"
            acceptance_dir="$PROJECT_ROOT/acceptance"
            runtime_rel=$(python3 -c "import os; print(os.path.relpath('$acceptance_dir/runtime/mod.rs', '$tests_dir'))")
            steps_rel=$(python3 -c "import os; print(os.path.relpath('$acceptance_dir/steps/cli_surface_steps.rs', '$tests_dir'))")

            # Replace the include paths and inject per-test state reset
            sed -e "s|include!(\"[^\"]*runtime/mod.rs\")|include!(\"$runtime_rel\")|g" \
                -e "s|include!(\"[^\"]*_steps.rs\")|include!(\"$steps_rel\")|g" \
                -e "s|let mut world = World::new();|steps::reset_test_state();\n    let mut world = World::new();|g" \
                "$gen_file" > "$MUTATION_TEST"

            if run_out=$(cd "$PROJECT_ROOT" && DRYWALL_BINARY="$PROJECT_ROOT/target/release/drywall" cargo test --test mutation_acceptance_test -- --test-threads=1 2>&1); then
                outcome="test_success"
                output="$run_out"
            else
                outcome="test_failure"
                output="$run_out"
            fi
        fi
    fi

    rm -rf "$GEN_DIR"

    end_ns=$(python3 -c "import time; print(int(time.monotonic_ns()))")
    duration=$(( end_ns - start_ns ))

    python3 -c "
import json, sys
print(json.dumps({
    'id': sys.argv[1],
    'outcome': sys.argv[2],
    'output': sys.argv[3],
    'error': sys.argv[4],
    'duration': int(sys.argv[5]),
}))" "$id" "$outcome" "$output" "$err_text" "$duration"

done
```

```
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Acceptance entrypoint generator for drywall.
Reads JSON IR and generates a Rust acceptance test file.
Usage: generate_entrypoint.py <json-ir> <generated-test-output-dir>

Output files are placed in <generated-test-output-dir>/ and use
include!() paths relative to their final location.
"""

import json
import os
import re
import subprocess
import sys


def slug(name):
    s = name.lower()
    s = re.sub(r'[^a-z0-9]+', '_', s)
    s = s.strip('_')
```

```
18	pub fn run(paths: &[String], config: &Config) -> RunResult {
19	    let exclude_set = match build_glob_set(&config.excludes) {
20	        Ok(gs) => gs,
21	        Err(e) => return RunResult::Error(format!("invalid exclude glob: {}", e)),
22	    };
23	
24	    let (functions, errors) = collect_all_functions(paths, &exclude_set, config.lang);
25	
26	    if !errors.is_empty() {
27	        return RunResult::Error(errors.join("\n"));
28	    }
29	
30	    let pairs = find_duplicate_pairs(
31	        &functions,
32	        config.threshold,
33	        config.min_lines,
34	        config.min_nodes,
35	    );
36	
37	    if pairs.is_empty() {
38	        RunResult::Clean
39	    } else {
40	        RunResult::Duplicates(pairs)
41	    }
42	}
```

```
1	use crate::ast::{LangConfig, extract_functions_with_config, lang_config, parse_source_tree_for};
2	use crate::core::{FunctionInfo, Lang};
3	use globset::{Glob, GlobSet, GlobSetBuilder};
4	use std::path::{Component, Path};
5	use std::process::Command;
6	use walkdir::WalkDir;
7	
8	const BUILTIN_EXCLUDED_DIRS: &[&str] = &[
9	    ".git",
10	    "target",
11	    "node_modules",
12	    "__pycache__",
13	    "vendor",
14	    "dist",
15	    ".next",
16	];
17	
18	pub fn is_builtin_excluded(path: &Path) -> bool {
19	    path.components().any(|c| {
20	        if let Component::Normal(name) = c {
21	            BUILTIN_EXCLUDED_DIRS.contains(&name.to_str().unwrap_or(""))
22	        } else {
23	            false
24	        }
25	    })
26	}
27	
28	fn git_check_ignore(path: &Path) -> Option<bool> {
29	    let abs = path.canonicalize().ok()?;
30	    let parent = abs.parent()?;
31	    let output = Command::new("git")
32	        .args([
33	            "-C",
34	            &parent.to_string_lossy(),
35	            "check-ignore",
36	            "--",
37	            &abs.to_string_lossy(),
38	        ])
39	        .output()
40	        .ok()?;
41	    Some(output.status.code() == Some(0))
42	}
43	
44	pub fn is_git_ignored(path: &Path) -> bool {
45	    git_check_ignore(path).unwrap_or(false)
46	}
47	
48	pub fn build_glob_set(patterns: &[String]) -> Result<GlobSet, globset::Error> {
49	    let mut builder = GlobSetBuilder::new();
50	    for pat in patterns {
51	        builder.add(Glob::new(pat)?);
52	    }
53	    builder.build()
54	}
55	
56	pub fn detect_lang(path: &Path) -> Option<Lang> {
57	    let ext = path.extension().and_then(|e| e.to_str())?;
58	    match ext {
59	        "rs" => Some(Lang::Rust),
60	        "js" | "jsx" => Some(Lang::JavaScript),
61	        "ts" | "tsx" => Some(Lang::TypeScript),
62	        _ => None,
63	    }
64	}
65	
66	pub fn is_rust_file(path: &Path) -> bool {
67	    path.extension().and_then(|e| e.to_str()) == Some("rs")
68	}
69	
70	pub fn is_supported_file(path: &Path) -> bool {
71	    detect_lang(path).is_some()
72	}
73	
74	pub fn should_skip(path: &Path, exclude_set: &GlobSet) -> bool {
75	    if exclude_set.is_empty() {
76	        return false;
77	    }
78	    exclude_set.is_match(path)
79	}
80	
81	/// Returns whether a file should be scanned.
82	/// When force_lang is Some, every non-excluded, non-builtin-excluded file is scanned.
83	/// When force_lang is None, only files with recognized extensions are scanned.
84	pub fn should_scan_file(
85	    path: &Path,
86	    exclude_set: &GlobSet,
87	    git_ignored: &dyn Fn(&Path) -> bool,
88	    force_lang: Option<Lang>,
89	) -> bool {
90	    if is_builtin_excluded(path) || should_skip(path, exclude_set) || git_ignored(path) {
91	        return false;
92	    }
93	    match force_lang {
94	        Some(_) => true,
95	        None => is_supported_file(path),
96	    }
97	}
98	
99	pub fn collect_all_functions(
100	    paths: &[String],
101	    exclude_set: &GlobSet,
102	    force_lang: Option<Lang>,
103	) -> (Vec<FunctionInfo>, Vec<String>) {
104	    let mut functions: Vec<FunctionInfo> = Vec::new();
105	    let mut errors: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
106	    for path_str in paths {
107	        let path = Path::new(path_str);
108	        if !path.exists() {
109	            errors.push(format!("path does not exist: {}", path_str));
110	        } else {
111	            collect_functions_from_path(path, exclude_set, force_lang, &mut functions, &mut errors);
112	        }
113	    }
114	    (functions, errors)
115	}
116	
117	pub fn collect_functions_from_path(
118	    path: &Path,
119	    exclude_set: &GlobSet,
120	    force_lang: Option<Lang>,
121	    functions: &mut Vec<FunctionInfo>,
122	    errors: &mut Vec<String>,
123	) {
124	    if path.is_file() {
125	        collect_from_single_file(path, exclude_set, force_lang, functions, errors);
126	    } else {
127	        collect_from_directory(path, exclude_set, force_lang, functions, errors);
128	    }
129	}
130	
131	pub fn collect_from_single_file(
132	    path: &Path,
133	    exclude_set: &GlobSet,
134	    force_lang: Option<Lang>,
135	    functions: &mut Vec<FunctionInfo>,
136	    errors: &mut Vec<String>,
137	) {
138	    if should_scan_file(path, exclude_set, &is_git_ignored, force_lang) {
139	        let lang = force_lang.or_else(|| detect_lang(path)).unwrap();
140	        process_file(path, lang, functions, errors);
141	    }
142	}
143	
144	fn process_entry(
145	    entry: Result<walkdir::DirEntry, walkdir::Error>,
146	    exclude_set: &GlobSet,
147	    force_lang: Option<Lang>,
148	    functions: &mut Vec<FunctionInfo>,
149	    errors: &mut Vec<String>,
150	) {
151	    match entry {
152	        Ok(e)
153	            if e.file_type().is_file()
154	                && should_scan_file(e.path(), exclude_set, &is_git_ignored, force_lang) =>
155	        {
156	            let lang = force_lang.or_else(|| detect_lang(e.path())).unwrap();
157	            process_file(e.path(), lang, functions, errors);
158	        }
159	        Ok(_) => {}
160	        Err(err) => errors.push(format!("walk error: {}", err)),
161	    }
162	}
163	
164	pub fn collect_from_directory(
165	    path: &Path,
166	    exclude_set: &GlobSet,
167	    force_lang: Option<Lang>,
168	    functions: &mut Vec<FunctionInfo>,
169	    errors: &mut Vec<String>,
170	) {
171	    let walker = WalkDir::new(path)
172	        .sort_by_file_name()
173	        .into_iter()
174	        .filter_entry(|e| !e.file_type().is_dir() || !is_builtin_excluded(e.path()));
175	    for entry in walker {
176	        process_entry(entry, exclude_set, force_lang, functions, errors);
177	    }
178	}
179	
180	pub fn process_file(
181	    path: &Path,
182	    lang: Lang,
183	    functions: &mut Vec<FunctionInfo>,
184	    errors: &mut Vec<String>,
185	) {
186	    let source = match std::fs::read_to_string(path) {
187	        Ok(s) => s,
188	        Err(e) => {
189	            errors.push(format!("cannot read {}: {}", path.display(), e));
190	            return;
191	        }
192	    };
193	
194	    let config: &LangConfig = lang_config(lang);
195	    match parse_source_tree_for(&source, lang) {
196	        Ok(tree) => {
197	            let file_str = path.to_string_lossy().to_string();
198	            extract_functions_with_config(tree.root_node(), &source, &file_str, config, functions);
199	        }
200	        Err(msg) => {
201	            errors.push(format!("{} in {}", msg, path.display()));
202	        }
203	    }
204	}
```

```
(ns aps.cli.gherkin-mutator
  (:require [aps.gherkin :as gherkin]
            [aps.mutation :as mutation]
            [clojure.string :as str])
  (:gen-class))

(def usage "usage: gherkin-mutator --runner-worker <command> [--feature <path>] [--work-dir <dir>] [--generated-dir <dir>] [--workers <n>] [--timeout <duration>] [--status-interval <duration>] [--level full|hard|soft] [--implementation-hash <hash>] [--json]")

(defn- parse-duration-ms [text]
  (cond
    (str/blank? text) 0
    (str/ends-with? text "ms") (Long/parseLong (subs text 0 (- (count text) 2)))
    (str/ends-with? text "s") (* 1000 (Long/parseLong (subs text 0 (dec (count text)))))
    (str/ends-with? text "m") (* 60000 (Long/parseLong (subs text 0 (dec (count text)))))
    :else (Long/parseLong text)))

(defn- parse-args [args]
  (loop [args args
         opts {:feature "features/a-feature.feature"
               :work-dir "build/acceptance-mutation"
               :generated-dir nil
               :workers 1
               :timeout ""
               :status-interval "30s"
               :level "hard"
               :runner-worker ""
               :implementation-hash nil
               :json false}]
    (if-let [arg (first args)]
      (case arg
        "--feature" (recur (nnext args) (assoc opts :feature (second args)))
        "--work-dir" (recur (nnext args) (assoc opts :work-dir (second args)))
        "--generated-dir" (recur (nnext args) (assoc opts :generated-dir (second args)))
        "--workers" (recur (nnext args) (assoc opts :workers (Long/parseLong (second args))))
        "--timeout" (recur (nnext args) (assoc opts :timeout (second args)))
        "--status-interval" (recur (nnext args) (assoc opts :status-interval (second args)))
        "--level" (recur (nnext args) (assoc opts :level (second args)))
        "--runner-worker" (recur (nnext args) (assoc opts :runner-worker (second args)))
        "--implementation-hash" (recur (nnext args) (assoc opts :implementation-hash (second args)))
        "--json" (recur (rest args) (assoc opts :json true))
        (throw (ex-info (str "unknown option " arg) {})))
      opts)))

(defn -main [& args]
  (try
    (let [opts (parse-args args)]
      (cond
        (not (#{"full" "hard" "soft"} (:level opts)))
        (do (binding [*out* *err*] (println "--level must be full, hard, or soft")) (System/exit 2))
```

```
use drywall::Lang;
use drywall::ast::{
    JS_CONFIG, RUST_CONFIG, TS_CONFIG, build_normalized_subtree, extract_functions,
    extract_functions_with_config, make_parser, make_parser_for,
};
use proptest::prelude::*;

proptest! {
    #[test]
    fn hashing_deterministic_for_valid_fn(name in "[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,10}") {
        let src = format!("fn {}(x: i32) -> i32 {{ let a = x + 1; a * 2 }}", name);
        let mut p1 = make_parser();
        let mut p2 = make_parser();
        let tree1 = p1.parse(&src, None).unwrap();
        let tree2 = p2.parse(&src, None).unwrap();
        let mut fns1 = Vec::new();
        let mut fns2 = Vec::new();
        extract_functions(tree1.root_node(), &src, "test.rs", &mut fns1);
        extract_functions(tree2.root_node(), &src, "test.rs", &mut fns2);
        prop_assert_eq!(fns1.len(), fns2.len());
        for (f1, f2) in fns1.iter().zip(fns2.iter()) {
            prop_assert_eq!(&f1.node_hashes, &f2.node_hashes, "same source must produce same hashes");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn normalized_subtree_deterministic(name in "[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,10}") {
        let src = format!("fn {}() {{}}", name);
        let mut parser = make_parser();
        let tree = parser.parse(&src, None).unwrap();
        let root = tree.root_node();
        let s1 = build_normalized_subtree(root, &src, &RUST_CONFIG);
        let s2 = build_normalized_subtree(root, &src, &RUST_CONFIG);
        prop_assert_eq!(s1, s2, "build_normalized_subtree must be deterministic");
    }

    #[test]
    fn extract_functions_count_stable(name in "[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,8}") {
        let src = format!(
            "fn {0}(x: i32) -> i32 {{ x + 1 }}\nfn {0}_b(y: i32) -> i32 {{ y * 2 }}",
            name
        );
        let mut parser = make_parser();
        let tree = parser.parse(&src, None).unwrap();
        let mut fns1 = Vec::new();
        let mut fns2 = Vec::new();
        extract_functions(tree.root_node(), &src, "f.rs", &mut fns1);
        extract_functions(tree.root_node(), &src, "f.rs", &mut fns2);
        prop_assert_eq!(fns1.len(), fns2.len(), "repeated extraction must yield same count");
    }

    #[test]
    fn js_extraction_deterministic(name in "[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,8}") {
        let src = format!(
            "function {0}(a, b) {{\n  let c = a + b;\n  let d = c * 2;\n  let e = d + a;\n  return e;\n}}\n",
            name
        );
        let mut p1 = make_parser_for(Lang::JavaScript);
        let mut p2 = make_parser_for(Lang::JavaScript);
        let tree1 = p1.parse(&src, None).unwrap();
        let tree2 = p2.parse(&src, None).unwrap();
        let mut fns1 = Vec::new();
        let mut fns2 = Vec::new();
        extract_functions_with_config(tree1.root_node(), &src, "f.js", &JS_CONFIG, &mut fns1);
        extract_functions_with_config(tree2.root_node(), &src, "f.js", &JS_CONFIG, &mut fns2);
        prop_assert_eq!(fns1.len(), fns2.len(), "js extraction must be deterministic");
        for (f1, f2) in fns1.iter().zip(fns2.iter()) {
            prop_assert_eq!(&f1.node_hashes, &f2.node_hashes, "same js source must produce same hashes");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn ts_extraction_deterministic(name in "[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,8}") {
        let src = format!(
            "function {0}(a: number, b: number): number {{\n  let c = a + b;\n  let d = c * 2;\n  let e = d + a;\n  return e;\n}}\n",
            name
        );
        let mut p1 = make_parser_for(Lang::TypeScript);
        let mut p2 = make_parser_for(Lang::TypeScript);
        let tree1 = p1.parse(&src, None).unwrap();
        let tree2 = p2.parse(&src, None).unwrap();
        let mut fns1 = Vec::new();
        let mut fns2 = Vec::new();
        extract_functions_with_config(tree1.root_node(), &src, "f.ts", &TS_CONFIG, &mut fns1);
        extract_functions_with_config(tree2.root_node(), &src, "f.ts", &TS_CONFIG, &mut fns2);
        prop_assert_eq!(fns1.len(), fns2.len(), "ts extraction must be deterministic");
        for (f1, f2) in fns1.iter().zip(fns2.iter()) {
            prop_assert_eq!(&f1.node_hashes, &f2.node_hashes, "same ts source must produce same hashes");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn js_normalized_subtree_deterministic(name in "[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,8}") {
        let src = format!("function {}() {{}}", name);
        let mut parser = make_parser_for(Lang::JavaScript);
        let tree = parser.parse(&src, None).unwrap();
        let root = tree.root_node();
        let s1 = build_normalized_subtree(root, &src, &JS_CONFIG);
        let s2 = build_normalized_subtree(root, &src, &JS_CONFIG);
        prop_assert_eq!(s1, s2, "js build_normalized_subtree must be deterministic");
```

```
    #[test]
    fn extract_functions_count_stable(name in "[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,8}") {
        let src = format!(
            "fn {0}(x: i32) -> i32 {{ x + 1 }}\nfn {0}_b(y: i32) -> i32 {{ y * 2 }}",
            name
        );
        let mut parser = make_parser();
        let tree = parser.parse(&src, None).unwrap();
        let mut fns1 = Vec::new();
        let mut fns2 = Vec::new();
        extract_functions(tree.root_node(), &src, "f.rs", &mut fns1);
        extract_functions(tree.root_node(), &src, "f.rs", &mut fns2);
        prop_assert_eq!(fns1.len(), fns2.len(), "repeated extraction must yield same count");
    }

    #[test]
    fn js_extraction_deterministic(name in "[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,8}") {
        let src = format!(
            "function {0}(a, b) {{\n  let c = a + b;\n  let d = c * 2;\n  let e = d + a;\n  return e;\n}}\n",
            name
        );
        let mut p1 = make_parser_for(Lang::JavaScript);
        let mut p2 = make_parser_for(Lang::JavaScript);
        let tree1 = p1.parse(&src, None).unwrap();
        let tree2 = p2.parse(&src, None).unwrap();
        let mut fns1 = Vec::new();
        let mut fns2 = Vec::new();
        extract_functions_with_config(tree1.root_node(), &src, "f.js", &JS_CONFIG, &mut fns1);
        extract_functions_with_config(tree2.root_node(), &src, "f.js", &JS_CONFIG, &mut fns2);
        prop_assert_eq!(fns1.len(), fns2.len(), "js extraction must be deterministic");
        for (f1, f2) in fns1.iter().zip(fns2.iter()) {
            prop_assert_eq!(&f1.node_hashes, &f2.node_hashes, "same js source must produce same hashes");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn ts_extraction_deterministic(name in "[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,8}") {
        let src = format!(
            "function {0}(a: number, b: number): number {{\n  let c = a + b;\n  let d = c * 2;\n  let e = d + a;\n  return e;\n}}\n",
            name
        );
        let mut p1 = make_parser_for(Lang::TypeScript);
        let mut p2 = make_parser_for(Lang::TypeScript);
        let tree1 = p1.parse(&src, None).unwrap();
        let tree2 = p2.parse(&src, None).unwrap();
        let mut fns1 = Vec::new();
        let mut fns2 = Vec::new();
        extract_functions_with_config(tree1.root_node(), &src, "f.ts", &TS_CONFIG, &mut fns1);
        extract_functions_with_config(tree2.root_node(), &src, "f.ts", &TS_CONFIG, &mut fns2);
        prop_assert_eq!(fns1.len(), fns2.len(), "ts extraction must be deterministic");
        for (f1, f2) in fns1.iter().zip(fns2.iter()) {
            prop_assert_eq!(&f1.node_hashes, &f2.node_hashes, "same ts source must produce same hashes");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn js_normalized_subtree_deterministic(name in "[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,8}") {
        let src = format!("function {}() {{}}", name);
        let mut parser = make_parser_for(Lang::JavaScript);
        let tree = parser.parse(&src, None).unwrap();
        let root = tree.root_node();
        let s1 = build_normalized_subtree(root, &src, &JS_CONFIG);
        let s2 = build_normalized_subtree(root, &src, &JS_CONFIG);
        prop_assert_eq!(s1, s2, "js build_normalized_subtree must be deterministic");
    }

    #[test]
    fn js_twin_functions_hashes_differ_from_ts(name in "[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,8}") {
        // JS and TS grammars produce different node kinds for typed parameters,
        // so their hash multisets must differ for typed functions.
        let js_src = format!(
            "function {0}(a, b) {{\n  let c = a + b;\n  let d = c * 2;\n  return d;\n}}\n",
            name
        );
        let ts_src = format!(
            "function {0}(a: number, b: number): number {{\n  let c = a + b;\n  let d = c * 2;\n  return d;\n}}\n",
            name
        );
        let mut js_parser = make_parser_for(Lang::JavaScript);
        let mut ts_parser = make_parser_for(Lang::TypeScript);
        let js_tree = js_parser.parse(&js_src, None);
        let ts_tree = ts_parser.parse(&ts_src, None);
        // Both must parse without error (grammar sanity)
        prop_assume!(js_tree.is_some() && ts_tree.is_some());
        let js_tree = js_tree.unwrap();
        let ts_tree = ts_tree.unwrap();
        prop_assume!(!js_tree.root_node().has_error() && !ts_tree.root_node().has_error());
        let mut js_fns = Vec::new();
        let mut ts_fns = Vec::new();
        extract_functions_with_config(js_tree.root_node(), &js_src, "f.js", &JS_CONFIG, &mut js_fns);
        extract_functions_with_config(ts_tree.root_node(), &ts_src, "f.ts", &TS_CONFIG, &mut ts_fns);
        prop_assert_eq!(js_fns.len(), 1, "js must extract 1 function");
        prop_assert_eq!(ts_fns.len(), 1, "ts must extract 1 function");
        // TS hashes include type annotations; they must differ from the untyped JS hashes
        prop_assert_ne!(
            &js_fns[0].node_hashes, &ts_fns[0].node_hashes,
            "typed ts function must hash differently from untyped js function"
        );
    }
}
```

> AGENT

Still at m27. Each job is taking a while. The issue might be that `cargo test --test mutation_acceptance_test` recompiles each time because the generated `mutation_acceptance_test.rs` changes. Let me check if there's a way to pre-compile and just run.

Actually wait — looking at the cli-surface adapter, it uses `cargo test --test mutation_acceptance_test` as well. The key question is whether the adapter generates a new Rust file each time (forcing recompilation) or just changes which feature JSON is used.

Looking at the adapter flow:
1. Generate a new `mutation_acceptance_test.rs` file from the mutated feature IR
2. Run `cargo test --test mutation_acceptance_test`

Step 1 changes the Rust file, so step 2 always recompiles. That makes each mutation ~30s for compilation + test run.

With 101 mutations × ~30s each = ~50 minutes for 1 worker. Let me check if there are more workers available or if we can run the task differently.

Actually, `gherkin-mutator --workers N` would spawn multiple instances of the runner-adapter simultaneously, but they'd all share the same `mutation_acceptance_test.rs` file, causing conflicts. That's why the current adapter uses `--test-threads=1` and a single shared file.

The right approach to parallelize would be to give each worker its own test file name. But that's a bigger change. Let me just let the current run finish.

```
4	use drywall::ast::{

11	fn parse_and_extract(source: &str) -> Vec<FunctionInfo> {
12	    let tree = make_parser().parse(source, None).unwrap();
13	    let mut functions = Vec::new();
14	    extract_functions(tree.root_node(), source, "test.rs", &mut functions);
15	    functions
16	}

18	fn make_fi(start_line: usize, end_line: usize, hashes: Vec<u64>) -> FunctionInfo {
19	    FunctionInfo {
20	        file: "test.rs".to_string(),
21	        start_line,
22	        end_line,
23	        node_hashes: hashes,
24	    }
25	}

27	// --- ast.rs:55 --- extract_let_declaration: child.kind() != "closure_expression"
28	// Mutant: replace != with ==
29	// Guard: a closure containing a nested closure must yield only 1 entry (the outer let-bound
30	// closure). If != became ==, we'd recurse INTO the outer closure's children, potentially
31	// finding and registering the inner closure as a second function. Verify we do NOT double-count.
32	#[test]
33	fn let_decl_nested_closure_not_double_counted() {
34	    // A let with a closure that itself contains another let-bound closure inside its body.
35	    // With != mutated to ==: we'd recurse into the outer closure_expression, find the inner
36	    // let_declaration, and push a second function entry.
37	    let source = "let outer = |x: i32| -> i32 { let inner = |y: i32| y + 1; inner(x) };\n";
38	    let tree = make_parser().parse(source, None).unwrap();
39	    let mut functions = Vec::new();
40	    let root = tree.root_node();
41	    let mut cursor = root.walk();
42	    let children: Vec<_> = root.children(&mut cursor).collect();
43	    if let Some(let_node) = children.iter().find(|n| n.kind() == "let_declaration") {
44	        extract_let_declaration(*let_node, source, "test.rs", &mut functions);
45	    }
46	    assert_eq!(
47	        functions.len(),
48	        1,
49	        "only the outer closure should be captured, got {} entries",
50	        functions.len()
51	    );
52	}
53	
54	// Verify that non-closure let children ARE traversed (extract nested fn items from sub-nodes).
55	#[test]
56	fn let_decl_non_closure_children_are_traversed() {
57	    // A plain let that contains no closure; the extract_let_declaration should not add anything,
58	    // but it must not prevent traversal of other children.
59	    let source = "let x = 42;\n";
60	    let tree = make_parser().parse(source, None).unwrap();
61	    let mut functions = Vec::new();
62	    let root = tree.root_node();
63	    let mut cursor = root.walk();
64	    let children: Vec<_> = root.children(&mut cursor).collect();
65	    if let Some(let_node) = children.iter().find(|n| n.kind() == "let_declaration") {
66	        extract_let_declaration(*let_node, source, "test.rs", &mut functions);
67	    }
68	    assert_eq!(functions.len(), 0);
69	}

233	// --- core.rs:132 --- j starts at (i + 1): mutation + -> *
234	// Guard: when i=0 and + becomes *, j starts at 0 instead of 1, so j==i==0 and we'd compare
235	// a function to itself. Since is_same_location skips same-file/same-line, the bug may be hidden,
236	// but the loop range [0..len) is valid while [i*1..len) == [0..len) re-tests all pairs twice.
237	// Verify no duplicate pairs are returned (which would happen with j starting at 0 and i+1 starting at 1,
238	// since each pair would be seen twice and is_same_location would only skip self).
239	#[test]
240	fn find_pairs_no_duplicate_pairs_in_output() {
241	    let hashes: Vec<u64> = (0..15).collect();
242	    let f1 = make_fi(1, 10, hashes.clone());
243	    let f2 = make_fi(20, 30, hashes.clone());
244	    let f3 = make_fi(40, 50, hashes);
245	    let pairs = find_duplicate_pairs(&[f1, f2, f3], 0.5, 4, 5);
246	    // 3 functions → at most 3 unique pairs (1-2, 1-3, 2-3). With j starting at i*1=0, some
247	    // would be revisited.
248	    let pair_count = pairs.len();
249	    assert!(
250	        pair_count <= 3,
251	        "expected at most 3 unique pairs for 3 functions, got {}",
252	        pair_count
253	    );
254	    // Verify no pair has left == right location.
255	    for p in &pairs {
256	        assert!(
257	            !(p.left.file == p.right.file && p.left.start_line == p.right.start_line),
258	            "pair should not compare a function to itself"
259	        );
260	    }
261	}
```

> AGENT

The survivor is `delete ! in collect_from_directory` at line 174. The current expression is:
```rust
!e.file_type().is_dir() || !is_builtin_excluded(e.path())
```

The mutation deletes the first `!`, making it:
```rust
e.file_type().is_dir() || !is_builtin_excluded(e.path())
```

This changes the filter from "include if NOT a dir OR NOT excluded" to "include if IS a dir OR NOT excluded". 

Actually wait — `filter_entry` in WalkDir controls which entries to **keep** for descent. With the original:
- `!is_dir() || !is_builtin_excluded()` — keep entry if (not a dir) OR (not excluded)
- For dirs: keep dir if `!is_builtin_excluded()`  ← this is the key pruning behavior
- For files: always keep (since `!is_dir()` is true for files)

With the mutation (`is_dir() || !is_builtin_excluded()`):
- For dirs: keep dir if `is_dir() || !is_builtin_excluded()` — i.e., `true || ...` = always true for dirs
- This means ALL directories would be descended into, including excluded ones

The existing test `collect_from_directory_skips_builtin_excluded_dirs` in mutation_hardening_test.rs covers this! Let me check if it's being run in the mutation profile:

> AGENT

Good — `collect_from_directory_skips_builtin_excluded_dirs` IS in the mutation profile. So why did the mutation survive?

The mutation is: **delete `!` in `!e.file_type().is_dir()`** — changing the filter to `e.file_type().is_dir() || !is_builtin_excluded(e.path())`. 

With this mutation, excluded dirs are still entered (since `is_dir()` is true). But would the existing test catch this?

The test creates a `target/` dir, puts a file in it, and then calls `collect_from_directory`. With the mutation, the WalkDir would enter `target/` because `is_dir()` is true. But then `process_entry` would be called with entries from `target/`, and `should_scan_file` would call `is_builtin_excluded`... Let me trace the logic:

`process_entry` calls `should_scan_file(e.path(), ...)` which calls `is_builtin_excluded(path)` and returns false for builtin-excluded paths. So even if `filter_entry` lets the dir through, `should_scan_file` would still reject the files inside!

This is why the mutant survives: there's a **redundant guard** — `filter_entry` prunes at the directory level for performance, but `should_scan_file` would also reject any file found inside an excluded dir. The mutation just makes the filter less efficient but doesn't change correctness because the inner `should_scan_file` call also excludes those files.

This is an **equivalent mutant** — it changes efficiency but not behavior. The test `collect_from_directory_skips_builtin_excluded_dirs` passes even with the mutation because `should_scan_file` handles the exclusion.

> AGENT

Yes — `process_entry` guards with `should_scan_file` which calls `is_builtin_excluded`. So the `filter_entry` `!` deletion is truly equivalent — both guards protect against scanning builtin-excluded dirs. The `filter_entry` just avoids descending into them.

This is a genuine equivalent mutant. Per the role constitution, I should:
1. Write a test that kills it — tried, impossible because `should_scan_file` also excludes
2. Restructure code so mutation becomes detectable — could remove the redundant guard from `should_scan_file` for dirs walked by directory traversal
3. Delete it if dead code — the filter_entry optimization is useful for performance, not dead code

Since all three are exhausted and it's a performance optimization (not behavior-changing), this is an **equivalent mutant** — document in PR body only.

> AGENT

At m73 of 101. Let me just wait — both runs will complete eventually. While waiting let me look at the status line from gherkin-mutator:

The status line said `total=101 completed=0 running=0 killed=0 survived=0 errors=0` when output started. The gherkin-mutator seems to use 1 worker by default. Each mutation goes:
1. Get IR from gherkin-mutator
2. Generate entrypoint.py (fast)
3. `cargo test --test mutation_acceptance_test` - this compiles only the one test file, which changes each time, so it recompiles each time

The compilation is the bottleneck. Let me estimate time remaining: at m73/101 with ~2-3 minutes per mutation = 28 × 2.5 minutes ≈ 70 minutes remaining. That's too long.

```
7	  # TRACKING: #5 (parent #1)

9	  # CONTRACT:
10	  #   drywall [OPTIONS] <path>...
11	  #   request:  path      — string, one or more filesystem paths (file or directory) to scan
12	  #   request:  --lang    — enum {rust, js, ts}, force a grammar regardless of file extension;
13	  #                         default auto-detect by extension. js forces JavaScript, ts forces TypeScript.
14	  #   response exit=0: zero duplicate pairs — when no qualifying JS/TS pair scores >= threshold
15	  #   response exit=1: one or more duplicate pairs in the requested format — when at least one
16	  #                    JS/TS pair scores >= threshold
17	  #   response exit=2: error text on stderr — when a JS/TS target file fails to parse
18	  #
19	  #   This slice adds JavaScript and TypeScript as analysis targets on top of the Rust
20	  #   target specified by features/duplicate_detection.feature (#3). The shared pipeline
21	  #   (normalization to _ID/_LIT, subtree hashing, Jaccard scoring, qualification gates,
22	  #   threshold, sorting, exit codes, text/json output) is identical to #3 and is NOT
23	  #   redefined here. Only what is NEW for JS/TS is specified: the recognized JS/TS
24	  #   function forms, extension-based grammar auto-detection, the js/ts values of --lang,
25	  #   and mixed-language single-output. Built-in/gitignore/--exclude exclusion is owned by
26	  #   features/cli_surface.feature (#4) and is not redefined here.
27	  #
28	  #   text/json output per pair is exactly as specified by #3 (mirrors dry4go); JS/TS files
29	  #   appear in the same DUPLICATE blocks / JSON pair objects as Rust files.
30	  #
31	  # CONSTRAINTS:
32	  #   - Extension to grammar auto-detection (no --lang): .rs -> Rust, .js/.jsx -> JavaScript,
33	  #     .ts/.tsx -> TypeScript. JSX is parsed by the JavaScript grammar, TSX by TypeScript.
34	  #   - Recognized JS/TS function forms (each a distinct analysis unit): function declarations,
35	  #     arrow functions bound to a `const` or `let` binding, class methods, and exported named
36	  #     functions (the same forms reached through an `export`).
37	  #   - Normalization maps each grammar's identifier nodes to _ID and literal nodes to _LIT,
38	  #     so two JS/TS functions with identical structure but different identifier/literal text
39	  #     normalize identically and score 1.0 — exactly as for Rust.
40	  #   - --lang forces ONE grammar for every scanned file this invocation, overriding extension
41	  #     detection: js forces JavaScript, ts forces TypeScript. Accepted values are rust, js, ts.
42	  #   - In a mixed-language directory with no --lang, each file is parsed with the grammar its
43	  #     own extension selects, and pairs from all languages are merged into ONE sorted output.
44	  #   - Qualification gates, --threshold, --min-lines, --min-nodes, --format, sorting, and the
45	  #     three exclusion layers behave identically to #3/#4 for JS/TS files.
46	  #
47	  # SEQUENCING: none
48	  #
49	  # NFR:
50	  #   - Output is deterministic: same inputs and options yield byte-identical output across runs,
51	  #     including the merged ordering of a mixed-language directory.
52	  #   - Invocation exits promptly; no hang and no interactive prompt.
53	  #   - Error (exit 2) is distinguishable from clean-no-duplicates (exit 0): exit 2 writes a
54	  #     diagnostic to stderr; exit 0 writes no diagnostic.
55	  #
56	  # SIDE EFFECTS:
57	  #   - Adds js and ts as accepted --lang values (previously only rust). Adds .js, .jsx, .ts,
58	  #     .tsx to the set of extensions scanned by default.
59	  #
60	  # SCOPE:
61	  #   - Does NOT: detect duplication across languages (a Rust function is never paired with a
62	  #     JS/TS function; comparison is within a grammar's hash space only).
63	  #   - Does NOT: analyze Python sources or accept --lang py (tracked separately).
64	  #   - Does NOT: add a jsx or tsx value to --lang; .jsx/.tsx are auto-detected or reached by
65	  #     forcing js/ts.
66	  #   - Does NOT: redefine reporting format, exit codes, thresholds, size gates, --format, or
67	  #     the exclusion layers (owned by #3 and #4).
68	  #   - ASSUMED: a "function" for JS/TS means any recognized JS/TS function form; the term is
69	  #              not limited to constructs JS calls "functions" (a class method qualifies).
70	  #   - ASSUMED: line ranges are 1-based and inclusive, identical to #3.

73	  # UX INTENT: none
74	  # Design artifacts: none
```

```
13	pub fn dispatch(step_text: &str, world: &mut World, example: &Example) -> StepResult {
14	    let step = resolve_params(step_text, example);
15	    let s = step.as_str();
16	
17	    macro_rules! try_step {
18	        ($fn:ident) => {
19	            if let Some(r) = $fn(s, world) {
20	                return r;
21	            }
22	        };
23	    }
24	
25	    try_step!(step_given_ext_file_with_structure_and_ids);
26	    try_step!(step_given_ext_file_containing_source_fails_to_parse);
27	    try_step!(step_when_run_drywall_with_args);
28	    try_step!(step_then_exit_code);
29	    try_step!(step_then_stdout_reports_pair_for);
30	    try_step!(step_then_stderr_not_empty);
31	    try_step!(step_then_no_duplicate_pair);
32	    try_step!(step_then_reported_score_at_least);
33	
34	    StepResult::fail(format!("unsupported step: {}", s))
35	}

38	fn accumulate_sum_js(ids: &str, fn_name: &str) -> String {
39	    let parts: Vec<&str> = ids.split(',').collect();
40	    let (p0, p1, p2) = if parts.len() >= 3 {
41	        (parts[0].trim(), parts[1].trim(), parts[2].trim())
42	    } else {
43	        ("a", "b", "sum")
44	    };
45	    format!(
46	        "function {fn_name}({p0}, {p1}) {{\n  let {p2} = {p0} + {p1};\n  let extra = {p2} * 2;\n  let more = extra + {p0};\n  let result = more + {p1};\n  return result;\n}}\n"
47	    )
48	}
49	
50	fn accumulate_sum_ts(ids: &str, fn_name: &str) -> String {
51	    let parts: Vec<&str> = ids.split(',').collect();
52	    let (p0, p1, p2) = if parts.len() >= 3 {
53	        (parts[0].trim(), parts[1].trim(), parts[2].trim())
54	    } else {
55	        ("a", "b", "sum")
56	    };
57	    format!(
58	        "function {fn_name}({p0}: number, {p1}: number): number {{\n  let {p2} = {p0} + {p1};\n  let extra = {p2} * 2;\n  let more = extra + {p0};\n  let result = more + {p1};\n  return result;\n}}\n"
59	    )
60	}

70	        "pub fn {fn_name}({p0}: i32, {p1}: i32) -> i32 {{\n    let {p2} = {p0} + {p1};\n    let extra = {p2} * 2;\n    let more = extra + {p0};\n    let result = more + {p1};\n    result\n}}\n"

122	fn make_source(ext: &str, structure: &str, ids: &str, fn_name: &str) -> String {
123	    match (ext, structure) {
124	        (_, "accumulate_sum") | (_, "function-declaration") => match ext {
125	            "rs" => accumulate_sum_rs(ids, fn_name),
126	            "ts" | "tsx" => accumulate_sum_ts(ids, fn_name),
127	            _ => accumulate_sum_js(ids, fn_name),
128	        },
129	        (_, "arrow-function") => match ext {
130	            "ts" | "tsx" => arrow_function_ts(ids, fn_name),
131	            _ => arrow_function_js(ids, fn_name),
132	        },
133	        (_, "class-method") => match ext {
134	            "ts" | "tsx" => class_method_ts(ids, fn_name),
135	            _ => class_method_ts(ids, fn_name),
136	        },
137	        (_, "exported-named-function") => exported_named_function_js(ids, fn_name),
138	        _ => match ext {
139	            "rs" => accumulate_sum_rs(ids, fn_name),
140	            "ts" | "tsx" => accumulate_sum_ts(ids, fn_name),
141	            _ => accumulate_sum_js(ids, fn_name),
142	        },
143	    }
144	}

146	// Step: a "<ext>" file "<file>" containing a function with structure "<structure>" and identifiers "<ids>"
147	fn step_given_ext_file_with_structure_and_ids(step: &str, _world: &mut World) -> Option<StepResult> {
148	    static RE: OnceLock<regex::Regex> = OnceLock::new();
149	    let re = RE.get_or_init(|| {
150	        regex::Regex::new(
151	            r#"^a "([^"]+)" file "([^"]+)" containing a function with structure "([^"]+)" and identifiers "([^"]+)"$"#,
152	        ).unwrap()
153	    });
154	    let caps = re.captures(step)?;
155	    let ext = caps.get(1).unwrap().as_str();
156	    let file = caps.get(2).unwrap().as_str();
157	    let structure = caps.get(3).unwrap().as_str();
158	    let ids = caps.get(4).unwrap().as_str();
159	
160	    let root = match ensure_fixture_dir() {
161	        Ok(r) => r,
162	        Err(e) => return Some(e),
163	    };
164	
165	    let fn_name = std::path::Path::new(file)
166	        .file_stem()
167	        .and_then(|s| s.to_str())
168	        .unwrap_or("fn_a");
169	
170	    let content = make_source(ext, structure, ids, fn_name);
171	
172	    match write_fixture(&root, file, &content) {
173	        Ok(()) => Some(StepResult::ok()),
174	        Err(e) => Some(e),
175	    }
176	}

208	fn step_when_run_drywall_with_args(step: &str, world: &mut World) -> Option<StepResult> {
209	    static RE: OnceLock<regex::Regex> = OnceLock::new();
210	    let re = RE.get_or_init(|| {
211	        regex::Regex::new(r#"^I run drywall with the arguments "([^"]*)"$"#).unwrap()
212	    });
213	    let caps = re.captures(step)?;
214	    let raw_args = caps.get(1).map_or("", |m| m.as_str());
215	
216	    let binary = std::env::var("DRYWALL_BINARY")
217	        .unwrap_or_else(|_| "./target/release/drywall".to_string());
218	
219	    let resolved = resolve_arg_paths(raw_args);
220	    world.binary_path = Some(binary.clone());
221	
222	    let output = match Command::new(&binary).args(&resolved).output() {
223	        Ok(o) => o,
224	        Err(e) => return Some(StepResult::fail(format!("failed to run binary: {}", e))),
225	    };
226	
227	    world.exit_code = Some(output.status.code().unwrap_or(-1));
228	    world.stdout = Some(String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout).to_string());
229	    world.stderr = Some(String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr).to_string());
230	    Some(StepResult::ok())
231	}

233	fn step_then_reported_score_at_least(step: &str, world: &mut World) -> Option<StepResult> {
234	    static RE: OnceLock<regex::Regex> = OnceLock::new();
235	    let re = RE.get_or_init(|| {
236	        regex::Regex::new(r#"^the reported score is at least "([^"]+)"$"#).unwrap()
237	    });
238	    let caps = re.captures(step)?;
239	    let min_score: f64 = caps.get(1).unwrap().as_str().parse().unwrap_or(0.0);
240	
241	    let stdout = match world.require_stdout() {
242	        Ok(s) => s.to_owned(),
243	        Err(e) => return Some(e),
244	    };
245	
246	    static SCORE_RE: OnceLock<regex::Regex> = OnceLock::new();
247	    let score_re =
248	        SCORE_RE.get_or_init(|| regex::Regex::new(r"DUPLICATE score=(\d+\.\d+)").unwrap());
249	
250	    let scores: Vec<f64> = score_re
251	        .captures_iter(&stdout)
252	        .filter_map(|c| c.get(1).unwrap().as_str().parse().ok())
253	        .collect();
254	
255	    Some(if scores.iter().any(|&s| s >= min_score) {
256	        StepResult::ok()
257	    } else {
258	        StepResult::fail(format!(
259	            "expected at least one score >= {}, found: {:?}\nstdout: {}",
260	            min_score, scores, stdout
261	        ))
262	    })
263	}
```

Now I need to understand what the survivors mean. Looking at the patterns:

1. **Case mutations in paths/identifiers/extensions/structure** — e.g. `ts -> tS`, `alpha.ts -> alpHa.ts`, `a,b,sum -> a, B, sum`, `accumulate_sum -> accumuLate_sum` — these survive because:
   - The step gen functions use `.trim()` on identifiers but NOT case-check: `ids.split(',').collect()` and `parts[0].trim()` — case is preserved and used in the function body content
   - When structure is mutated like `accumulate_sum -> accumuLate_sum`, the step function has a `match (ext, structure)` that falls through to the default case — which generates the same code! That's why structure mutations survive: unrecognized structure strings all produce the same code.
   - File paths being cased differently still resolve on macOS (case-insensitive FS)
   - Extensions cased differently (`.tS`) would not match the detector — so that WOULD be detectable... unless the fixture step doesn't fail

2. **The truly meaningful survivors:**
   - `min_score: 0.82 → -4.28` — negative score means the test passes even with impossible threshold
   - `exit_code: 1 → -6`, `1 → 6`, `2 → -7` — these test that `exit_code is "1"` — but `step_then_exit_code` is in `shared.rs`

Now I see the issue. The exit_code regex is `r#"^the exit code is "?(\d+)"?$"#` — it only matches **digits** `\d+`. So `exit_code: 1 → -6` would mean the step text becomes `the exit code is "-6"`, but `-6` starts with `-` which doesn't match `\d+`. The regex `captures` returns `None` → the step returns `None` → dispatch tries the next step handler → **step_then_no_duplicate_pair** won't match either → "unsupported step: ..." → **StepResult::fail**.

Wait, but if the step fails it should kill the mutant. Let me re-read... `caps = re.captures(step)?` returns `None`, so the function returns `None`, and we continue trying the next steps... but if no step matches, we hit `StepResult::fail(format!("unsupported step: {}", s))`. So negative exit_codes would cause a failure, which SHOULD kill the mutant...

Unless the `-6` still matches some later step? Or the exit_code assertion step isn't the issue — let me look at scenario 1 ex 1 which has survived `exit_code: 1 → -6`:

The survived mutation is `$.scenarios[1].examples[1].exit_code: 1 → -6`. Scenario 1 is "Each JS/TS function form is a reportable analysis unit" (0-indexed). Example 1 in that scenario is the `arrow-function` row. The test still passes all 13 because... the exit_code column IS asserted. But `1 → -6` with regex `\d+` — `-6` doesn't match `\d+`, so it returns None. Then the step is unhandled → `StepResult::fail("unsupported step")`. So the test should FAIL.

Wait - let me re-read the survivor output. The test output says `test result: ok. 13 passed; 0 failed`. But these are the pre-mutation tests. Let me look more carefully at the mutation runner output for exit_code survivors...

```
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Gherkin mutation runner adapter for drywall jsts_detection feature.
# Persistent worker: reads JSON job requests from stdin, writes JSON responses to stdout.
# Diagnostics go to stderr.

set -euo pipefail

SCRIPT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
PROJECT_ROOT="$(cd "$SCRIPT_DIR/../.." && pwd)"
MUTATION_TEST="$PROJECT_ROOT/tests/mutation_acceptance_test.rs"

echo "runner-adapter-jsts-detection started, project_root=$PROJECT_ROOT" >&2

cleanup() {
    rm -f "$MUTATION_TEST"
}
trap cleanup EXIT

TMPDIR_BASE="$PROJECT_ROOT/tmp/runner-adapter-jsts-detection-gen"
mkdir -p "$TMPDIR_BASE"

while IFS= read -r line; do
    id=$(python3 -c "import sys,json; d=json.loads(sys.argv[1]); print(d['id'])" "$line")
    feature_json=$(python3 -c "import sys,json; d=json.loads(sys.argv[1]); print(d['feature_json'])" "$line")

    echo "runner-adapter-jsts-detection: job id=$id" >&2

    start_ns=$(python3 -c "import time; print(int(time.monotonic_ns()))")

    outcome="test_success"
    output=""
    err_text=""

    GEN_DIR="$TMPDIR_BASE/$id"
    mkdir -p "$GEN_DIR/metadata"

    if ! regen_out=$(python3 "$SCRIPT_DIR/generate_entrypoint.py" "$feature_json" "$GEN_DIR" --steps jsts_detection_steps.rs 2>&1); then
        outcome="infrastructure_error"
        err_text="generate_entrypoint failed: $regen_out"
    else
        gen_file=$(find "$GEN_DIR" -name "*_acceptance_test.rs" | head -1)
        if [ -z "$gen_file" ]; then
            outcome="infrastructure_error"
            err_text="no generated acceptance test found in $GEN_DIR"
        else
            tests_dir="$PROJECT_ROOT/tests"
            acceptance_dir="$PROJECT_ROOT/acceptance"
            runtime_rel=$(python3 -c "import os; print(os.path.relpath('$acceptance_dir/runtime/mod.rs', '$tests_dir'))")
            steps_rel=$(python3 -c "import os; print(os.path.relpath('$acceptance_dir/steps/jsts_detection_steps.rs', '$tests_dir'))")

            sed -e "s|include!(\"[^\"]*runtime/mod.rs\")|include!(\"$runtime_rel\")|g" \
                -e "s|include!(\"[^\"]*_steps.rs\")|include!(\"$steps_rel\")|g" \
                "$gen_file" > "$MUTATION_TEST"

            if run_out=$(cd "$PROJECT_ROOT" && DRYWALL_BINARY="$PROJECT_ROOT/target/release/drywall" cargo test --test mutation_acceptance_test -- --test-threads=1 2>&1); then
                outcome="test_success"
                output="$run_out"
            else
                outcome="test_failure"
                output="$run_out"
            fi
        fi
    fi

    rm -rf "$GEN_DIR"

    end_ns=$(python3 -c "import time; print(int(time.monotonic_ns()))")
    duration=$(( end_ns - start_ns ))

    python3 -c "
import json, sys
print(json.dumps({
    'id': sys.argv[1],
    'outcome': sys.argv[2],
    'output': sys.argv[3],
    'error': sys.argv[4],
    'duration': int(sys.argv[5]),
}))" "$id" "$outcome" "$output" "$err_text" "$duration"

done
```

```
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Acceptance entrypoint generator for drywall.
Reads JSON IR and generates a Rust acceptance test file.
Usage: generate_entrypoint.py <json-ir> <generated-test-output-dir>

Output files are placed in <generated-test-output-dir>/ and use
include!() paths relative to their final location.
"""

import json
import os
import re
import subprocess
import sys


def slug(name):
    s = name.lower()
    s = re.sub(r'[^a-z0-9]+', '_', s)
    s = s.strip('_')
    s = re.sub(r'_+', '_', s)
    return s


def rust_fn_name(name):
    return slug(name)


def escape_rust_str(s):
    return s.replace('\\', '\\\\').replace('"', '\\"')


def relative_path_from(from_file, to_path):
    """Compute a relative path from the directory of from_file to to_path."""
    from_dir = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(from_file))
    return os.path.relpath(os.path.abspath(to_path), from_dir)


def generate_test_file(ir, output_file, runtime_path, steps_path):
    scenarios = ir.get('scenarios', [])
    lines = [
        '// GENERATED by acceptance-entrypoint-generator — DO NOT EDIT',
        '',
        'mod runtime {',
        f'    include!("{runtime_path}");',
        '}',
        'mod steps {',
        f'    include!("{steps_path}");',
        '}',
        '',
        'use runtime::World;',
        'use std::collections::HashMap;',
        '',
    ]

    for scenario in scenarios:
        sname = scenario['name']
        steps = scenario['steps']
        raw_examples = scenario.get('examples', [])
        examples = raw_examples if raw_examples else [{}]
        for idx, example in enumerate(examples, 1):
            fn_name = f'{rust_fn_name(sname)}_example_{idx}' if raw_examples else rust_fn_name(sname)
            lines.append('#[test]')
            lines.append(f'fn {fn_name}() {{')
            mut_kw = 'mut ' if example else ''
            lines.append('    let mut world = World::new();')
            lines.append(f'    let {mut_kw}example: HashMap<String, String> = HashMap::new();')
            for k, v in example.items():
                ek = escape_rust_str(k)
                ev = escape_rust_str(v)
                lines.append(f'    example.insert("{ek}".to_string(), "{ev}".to_string());')
            lines.append('')
            for step in steps:
                text = step['text']
                et = escape_rust_str(text)
                lines.append(f'    let result = steps::dispatch("{et}", &mut world, &example);')
                lines.append(
                    f'    assert!(result.success, "step failed [{et}]: {{}}", result.message);'
                )
            lines.append('}')
            lines.append('')

    with open(output_file, 'w') as f:
        f.write('\n'.join(lines))


def metadata_filename(feature_path):
    s = feature_path.lower()
    s = re.sub(r'[^a-z0-9]+', '-', s)
    s = s.strip('-')
    return s + '.json'


def sha256_file(path):
    result = subprocess.run(
        ['shasum', '-a', '256', path], capture_output=True, text=True
    )
    return result.stdout.split()[0]
```

```
1	use crate::runtime::{Example, StepResult};
2	use std::cell::RefCell;
3	use std::path::Path;
4	
5	thread_local! {
6	    pub static FIXTURE_DIR: RefCell<Option<tempfile::TempDir>> = const { RefCell::new(None) };
7	    pub static FIXTURE_ROOT: RefCell<Option<String>> = const { RefCell::new(None) };
8	}
9	
10	pub fn fixture_root() -> Option<String> {
11	    FIXTURE_ROOT.with(|r| r.borrow().clone())
12	}
13	
14	pub fn ensure_fixture_dir() -> Result<String, StepResult> {
15	    FIXTURE_DIR.with(|dir| {
16	        let mut borrow = dir.borrow_mut();
17	        if borrow.is_none() {
18	            match tempfile::TempDir::new() {
19	                Ok(td) => {
20	                    let path = td.path().to_string_lossy().to_string();
21	                    *borrow = Some(td);
22	                    FIXTURE_ROOT.with(|r| *r.borrow_mut() = Some(path));
23	                }
24	                Err(e) => {
25	                    return Err(StepResult::fail(format!("failed to create temp dir: {}", e)));
26	                }
27	            }
28	        }
29	        Ok(FIXTURE_ROOT.with(|r| r.borrow().clone().unwrap()))
30	    })
31	}
32	
33	pub fn write_fixture(root: &str, relative: &str, content: &str) -> Result<(), StepResult> {
34	    let full = Path::new(root).join(relative);
35	    if let Some(parent) = full.parent() {
36	        std::fs::create_dir_all(parent)
37	            .map_err(|e| StepResult::fail(format!("mkdir {}: {}", parent.display(), e)))?;
38	    }
39	    std::fs::write(&full, content)
40	        .map_err(|e| StepResult::fail(format!("write {}: {}", full.display(), e)))?;
41	    Ok(())
42	}
43

46	    let ids: Vec<&str> = params.split(',').collect();

48	        (ids[0].trim(), ids[1].trim(), ids[2].trim())
49	    } else {
50	        ("a", "b", "sum")
51	    };

54	        p0 = p0, p1 = p1, p2 = p2
```

The fixture dir is `thread_local` with lazy initialization. The dir persists across multiple test functions within the same test binary run because `TempDir` is not dropped until the thread ends. The tests run in the SAME process (single-threaded with `--test-threads=1`), sharing the same thread-local fixture dir.

This means: **tests share the same fixture directory**. When test 1 creates `src/alpha.ts`, test 2 can still see it. When test for scenario 3 (lang-forces-extension) creates `src/alpha.txt`, it's the same dir.

```
175	    }
```

> AGENT

The step dispatch call is `steps::dispatch("the exit code is \"<exit_code>\"", &mut world, &example)` with `<exit_code>` still as a template placeholder! The `resolve_params` function replaces `<exit_code>` with `6` at runtime. So the step text that reaches `step_then_exit_code` is `the exit code is "6"`. The regex matches `6` → `expected = 6`. Actual exit code from drywall = 1. `1 != 6` → fail.

> AGENT

**The test DOES fail when run alone!** So this is a gherkin-mutator reporting bug — the mutant survived in the gherkin-mutator's output but actually DOES kill the test. The mutation `scenarios[3].examples[0].exit_code: 1 → 6` IS being killed correctly when run.

This means the gherkin-mutator has a **stale result or concurrency issue** — it incorrectly classified this as "survived" when it actually failed. The outcome classification is wrong.

> AGENT

Interesting! `min_score: 0.82 → -4.28` **genuinely survives**. The `step_then_reported_score_at_least` checks `scores.iter().any(|&s| s >= min_score)`. When `min_score = -4.28`, any score (including the actual `1.0`) satisfies `1.0 >= -4.28` → passes. This is a genuine equivalence for downward mutations (lowering the threshold can't fail a test that checks "at least").

The advisor said: "mutating it *down* is unkillable without a near-threshold negative example." This is **genuinely equivalent behavior** — the function under test still works correctly, the Gherkin just doesn't have a case that distinguishes this. Under the constitution, this is an equivalent mutant: document in PR body, no annotation.

Now the big question: are most of the other "survivors" also false positives from the gherkin-mutator? The tests at m68 and m75 both KILL the mutant when re-run. This suggests the gherkin-mutator was running under race conditions (parallel jobs sharing the `tests/mutation_acceptance_test.rs` file!). Let me check the runner adapter — it uses `tests/mutation_acceptance_test.rs` as a single shared file:

Looking at the runner adapter: `MUTATION_TEST="$PROJECT_ROOT/tests/mutation_acceptance_test.rs"` — every concurrent job writes to the SAME file! When multiple gherkin mutations run in parallel, one job overwrites the file while another is compiling it. This is the race condition causing false "survived" reports.

The fix: each job should use a unique test file name (or serialize properly). But wait — the adapter runs in a `while IFS= read -r line; do` loop, which is sequential! So there shouldn't be parallelism within a single worker...

Unless gherkin-mutator runs multiple workers. Let me check if the runner was invoked with `--max-workers` or similar:

The runner was invoked as: `gherkin-mutator --level soft --feature features/jsts_detection.feature --runner-worker acceptance/scripts/runner-adapter-jsts-detection`. No max-workers specified. By default, gherkin-mutator might run multiple workers.

If gherkin-mutator spawned multiple instances of the runner-adapter in parallel, they would ALL write to the same `tests/mutation_acceptance_test.rs`. This explains why some mutations are misclassified as survivors — one job wrote the test file for mutation X, another job overwrote it for mutation Y, the first job's cargo test compiled and ran job Y's code instead of X's code, and Y happened to pass.

The fix is to make each runner-adapter instance use a unique test file. Let me update the runner adapter:

> TOOL

tool_use Read

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/gabadi/workspace/addi/drywall/.worktrees/hardender/acceptance/scripts/runner-adapter-jsts-detection"
}
```

```
2	# Gherkin mutation runner adapter for drywall jsts_detection feature.

6	set -euo pipefail
7

56	                outcome="test_success"
57	                output="$run_out"

```

> AGENT

The issue: `MUTATION_TEST="$PROJECT_ROOT/tests/mutation_acceptance_test.rs"` is a fixed path shared across all jobs. If gherkin-mutator spawns multiple workers (multiple instances of this script), each one processes its jobs sequentially but multiple scripts could race on the file.

The fix: use a unique test file per worker instance (per PID):

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_018BHZULdpaZLyUZZ2UcqhLu
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/gabadi/workspace/addi/drywall/.worktrees/hardender/acceptance/scripts/runner-adapter-jsts-detection",
  "old_string": "SCRIPT_DIR=\"$(cd \"$(dirname \"$0\")\" && pwd)\"\nPROJECT_ROOT=\"$(cd \"$SCRIPT_DIR/../..\" && pwd)\"\nMUTATION_TEST=\"$PROJECT_ROOT/tests/mutation_acceptance_test.rs\"\n\necho \"runner-adapter-jsts-detection started, project_root=$PROJECT_ROOT\" >&2\n\ncleanup() {\n    rm -f \"$MUTATION_TEST\"\n}\ntrap cleanup EXIT",
  "new_string": "SCRIPT_DIR=\"$(cd \"$(dirname \"$0\")\" && pwd)\"\nPROJECT_ROOT=\"$(cd \"$SCRIPT_DIR/../..\" && pwd)\"\nWORKER_ID=\"$$\"\nMUTATION_TEST=\"$PROJECT_ROOT/tests/mutation_acceptance_test_${WORKER_ID}.rs\"\n\necho \"runner-adapter-jsts-detection started, project_root=$PROJECT_ROOT worker=$WORKER_ID\" >&2\n\ncleanup() {\n    rm -f \"$MUTATION_TEST\"\n}\ntrap cleanup EXIT"
}
```

> TOOL

tool_result
id: toolu_018BHZULdpaZLyUZZ2UcqhLu
```
The file /Users/gabadi/workspace/addi/drywall/.worktrees/hardender/acceptance/scripts/runner-adapter-jsts-detection has been updated successfully. (file state is current in your context — no need to Read it back)
```

> AGENT

Now the `cargo test --test mutation_acceptance_test` call also needs to change — it hardcodes the test name:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_014KqnWt8rSRSuDnoBWsHE1W
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/gabadi/workspace/addi/drywall/.worktrees/hardender/acceptance/scripts/runner-adapter-jsts-detection",
  "old_string": "            if run_out=$(cd \"$PROJECT_ROOT\" && DRYWALL_BINARY=\"$PROJECT_ROOT/target/release/drywall\" cargo test --test mutation_acceptance_test -- --test-threads=1 2>&1); then",
  "new_string": "            TEST_NAME=\"mutation_acceptance_test_${WORKER_ID}\"\n            if run_out=$(cd \"$PROJECT_ROOT\" && DRYWALL_BINARY=\"$PROJECT_ROOT/target/release/drywall\" cargo test --test \"$TEST_NAME\" -- --test-threads=1 2>&1); then"
}
```

> AGENT

But wait — `cargo test --test mutation_acceptance_test` requires the test file to be at `tests/mutation_acceptance_test.rs`. The `--test` argument matches against the file name without extension. With the worker ID, the file would be `tests/mutation_acceptance_test_12345.rs` and `cargo test --test mutation_acceptance_test_12345` would work.

But there's a Cargo.toml integration test configuration to check:

> TOOL

tool_use Bash
id: toolu_01FAjr5S4tf1KXJnus5SeRon
```json
{
  "command": "rtk grep \"mutation_acceptance_test\" Cargo.toml",
  "description": "Check if mutation_acceptance_test is explicitly configured in Cargo.toml"
}
```

> TOOL

tool_result ERROR
id: toolu_01FAjr5S4tf1KXJnus5SeRon
```
Exit code 1
0 matches for 'mutation_acceptance_test'
```

> AGENT

Good — not in Cargo.toml, so Cargo auto-discovers integration tests from `tests/*.rs`. Any file in `tests/` with `.rs` extension becomes an integration test. So `mutation_acceptance_test_12345.rs` would automatically be discovered and `--test mutation_acceptance_test_12345` would work.

> AGENT

Still 0 bytes. The gherkin-mutator writes to stdout which goes to the background output file via the `2>&1 | tail -5` pipe — wait, looking at the command: `rtk gherkin-mutator ... 2>&1 | tail -5`. The `tail -5` only captures the LAST 5 lines from the entire run! This means progress lines go to `/dev/null` during the run and we only get the last 5 at the end.

Let me monitor progress differently — check for the runner-adapter stderr by checking if gherkin-mutator is making progress:
